\appendix

\section{Extension calculus cheatsheet}

We resume many results used in the calculus of extensions. We work in a virtual equipment $\bb{X}$, and every type is implicit.

\begin{itemize}
    \item $B(f, 1) \odot p \odot C(1, g) \simeq p(f, g)$
        \hfill Theorem \ref{th:comp_rest}
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}
    Definition \ref{def:co_lim}
\end{center}
\begin{multicols}{2}
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $X(\colim^{\vec{p}}f, 1) \simeq X(f, 1) \rift \vec{p}$
        \item $X(1, \lim{}^{\vec{p}}f) \simeq \vec{p} \rext X(1, f)$
    \end{itemize}
\end{multicols}

\begin{center}
    Definition \ref{def:pointwise}
\end{center}

\begin{multicols}{2}
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $Y(j \plext f, 1) \simeq Y(f, 1) \rift X(j, 1)$
        \item $Y(f\plift j, 1) \simeq Z(f, 1) \rext Z(j, 1) \simeq Z(j, f)$
        \item $Y(1, j\prext f) \simeq X(1, j) \rext Y(1, f)$
        \item $Y(1, f\prift j) \simeq Z(1, j) \rift Z(1, f) \simeq Z(f, j)$
    \end{itemize}
\end{multicols}

\begin{center}
    Lemma \ref{lem:ext_lift}
\end{center}

\begin{multicols}{2}
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $q \rift (p_1, \cdots, p_m) \simeq q \rift (p_1 \odot_L \cdots \odot_L p_i ,\cdots, p_m)$
        \item $q \rift (p_1, \cdots, p_m) \simeq q \rift (p_1, \cdots, p_i \odot \cdots \odot p_j, \cdots, p_m)$
        \item $(q \rift (p_1, \cdots, p_i)) \rift (p_{i+1}, \cdots, p_m) \simeq q \rift(p_1, \cdots, p_m)$
        \item $(q \rift \vec{p})(y, x) \simeq q(1, x) \rift (p_1, \cdots, p_m(1, y))$
        \item $(p_1, \cdots, p_m) \rext q \simeq (p_1, \cdots, p_i \odot_R \cdots \odot_R p_m) \rext q$
        \item $(p_1, \cdots, p_m) \rext q \simeq (p_1, \cdots, p_i \odot \cdots \odot p_j, \cdots, p_m) \rext q$
        \item $(p_1, \cdots, p_i) \rext ((p_{i+1}, \cdots, p_m) \rext q) \simeq (p_1, \cdots, p_m) \rext q$
        \item $(\vec{p} \rext q)(z, y) \simeq (p_1(y, 1), \cdots, p_m)\rext q(z, 1)$
    \end{itemize}
\end{multicols}
\vspace{-2\baselineskip+2\parsep}
$$\bullet\hspace{.5em}(\vec{p} \rext q) \rift \vec{r} \simeq \vec{p} \rext (q \rift \vec{r})$$

\begin{itemize}
    \item $\psh\PP A(\widearc{p}, \widearc{q}) \simeq q \rift p$
        \hfill Proposition \ref{prop:rift_psh}
    \item $\widearc{p(1, f)} = \widearc{p}\circ f$
\end{itemize}

\clearpage

\section{Subjects left untouched}
\label{app:future}

Quite a few subjects were left untouched, or at least not studied enough to appear in the main text of this thesis. In no particular order:

\begin{itemize}
    \item $\begin{tikzcd}
            \ca O  : \Ind A \ar[r, bend left=15, ""{name=O, below}] & \ar[l, bend left=15, ""{name=Spec, above}] \Pro A : \Spec \ar[from=O, to=Spec, adj]
        \end{tikzcd}$. According to \cite{diliberticodensity}, the Isbell adjunction restricts under certain hypothesis to an adjunction between $\Ind$ and $\Pro$. This was the motivation for Theorem \ref{th:isbell_restriction}, as I wanted to find a good class of distributors that would build both $\Ind A$ as a presheaf object and $\Pro A$ as a copresheaf object. However, the distributors $A\dfrom A$ complicate things, and such a class cannot be created. A possible solution could be considering \textit{relative presheaf objects} associated to an inclusion $(\bb X, \PP) \hookrightarrow (\bb X', \PP')$ of virtual equipments equipped with a class of distributors; but it wasn't explored.
    \item Idempotency for relative monads. This is studied in \cite{arkor2025idempotencerelativemonads}, and the details in virtual equipments are left for the reader to write. I had to scrap the subsection where this was done because there wasn't enough to justify its existence.
    \item Many generalizations of monads. The simplest one is a monad relative to a distributor, but there's also polymonads.
    \item Many generalizations of adjunctions. The most general one I could write was as a diagram
        \[\begin{tikzcd}
            B & \ar[l, loose, "I"', ""{anchor=center,name=I}] C \ar[d, "r"]\\
            \ar[u, "\ell"] A & \ar[l, loose, "J", ""{anchor=center,name=J}] D
            \ar[from=I, to=J, phantom, "\dashv"{anchor=center}]
        \end{tikzcd}\]
        With $I(\ell, 1) \simeqq J(1, r)$. Taking $I = 1$ and $J = D(j, 1)$ we get relative adjunctions while $I = B(1, i)$ and $J = 1$ gives relative coadjunctions. The characterization with unit/counit gave me a few problems but it should look like $\eta : J \To I(\ell, 1) \odot D(r, 1)$ and $\varepsilon : B(1, \ell), J(1, r) \To I$. This is probably to general to be useful.
    \item Constructions I couldn't axiomatize properly on in time: Grothendieck construction, totality as studied in \cite{koudenburg2024formalcategorytheoryaugmented}, weak equivalences as studied in \cite{betti_weak}, and an in depth study of the virtual equipment of module and of the virtual equipment of metric spaces. The theory of weak equivalences has interesting applications to Morita theory and Cauchy completion.
    \item The subsection on fibred categories: there wasn't a lot of litterature apart from the basics in \cite{borceux2} and \cite{benaboudistributorsfibrations}. Most importantly, I lacked time, organization, and space in this already too long thesis.
\end{itemize}

Finally, a lot of things could be said about (co)tabulators. It is possible to define them in a virtual equipment as a cell, following \cite{pare_yoneda} or \cite{koudenburg2024formalcategorytheoryaugmented}.

\[\begin{tikzcd}
    & \ar[dl, "\dom"'] \floor{J} \ar[dr, "\cod"] &\\
    A &{}& \ar[ll, loose, "J"] B
    \ar[from=1-2, to=2-2, phantom, "\text{tab}"{anchor=center}]
\end{tikzcd}\]

With two axioms

\begin{itemize}
    \item[(T1)] Every cell as on the left factorizes through $\floor J$ as on the right:
        \[\begin{tikzcd}
            & \ar[dl] X \ar[dr] &\\
            A &{}& \ar[ll, loose, "J"] B
            \ar[from=1-2, to=2-2, phantom, "\xi"{anchor=center}]
        \end{tikzcd}
        \hspace{1em}=\hspace{1em}
        \begin{tikzcd}
            & X \ar[d, "\overline\xi"] &\\
            & \ar[dl, "\dom"'] \floor J \ar[dr, "\cod"] & \\
            A &{}& \ar[ll, loose, "J"]
            \ar[from=2-2, to=3-2, phantom, "\text{tab}"{anchor=center}]
        \end{tikzcd}\]
    \item[(T2)] For every $\alpha_0, \alpha_1, \xi_0, \xi_1$ and $p, f, g, h, i$ such that
        \[\begin{tikzcd}
            \ar[d, "f"'] X & \ar[l, loose, "p"', ""{anchor=center,name=t}] Y \ar[d, "g"{description}] \ar[dr, "h"] &\\
            A & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=b1}] A & \ar[l, loose, "J", ""{anchor=center,name=b2}] B
            \ar[from=t, to=b1, phantom, "\xi_0"{anchor=center}] \ar[from=1-2, to=b2, phantom, "\alpha_0"{anchor=center}]
        \end{tikzcd}
        \hspace{1em}=\hspace{1em}
        \begin{tikzcd}
            & \ar[dl, "f"'] X \ar[d, "i"{description}] & \ar[l, loose, "p"', ""{anchor=center,name=t}] Y \ar[d, "h"]\\
            A & \ar[l, loose, "J", ""{anchor=center,name=b1}] B & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=b2}] B
            \ar[from=1-2, to=b1, phantom, "\alpha_1"{anchor=center}] \ar[from=t, to=b2, phantom, "\xi_1"{anchor=center}]
        \end{tikzcd}\]
        There is a unique $\xi$ such that
        \[\begin{tikzcd}
            \ar[d, "x"'] X & \ar[l, loose, "p"', ""{anchor=center,name=t}] Y \ar[d, "y"]\\
            \ar[d, "\cod"'] \floor{J} & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=m}] \floor{J} \ar[d, "\cod"]\\
            B & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=b}] B
            \ar[from=t, to=m, phantom, "\xi"{anchor=center}] \ar[from=m, to=b, phantom, "1_{\cod}"]
        \end{tikzcd}
        \hspace{1em}=\hspace{1em}
        \begin{tikzcd}
            \ar[d, "i"'] X & \ar[l, loose, "p"', ""{anchor=center,name=t}] Y \ar[d, "h"]\\
            B & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=b}] B
            \ar[from=t, to=m, phantom, "\xi_1"{anchor=center}]
        \end{tikzcd}
        \hspace{2em};\hspace{2em}
        \begin{tikzcd}
            \ar[d, "f"'] X & \ar[l, loose, "p"', ""{anchor=center,name=t}] Y \ar[d, "g"]\\
            A & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=b}] A
            \ar[from=t, to=m, phantom, "\xi_0"{anchor=center}]
        \end{tikzcd}
        \hspace{1em}=\hspace{1em}
        \begin{tikzcd}
            \ar[d, "x"'] X & \ar[l, loose, "p"', ""{anchor=center,name=t}] Y \ar[d, "y"]\\
            \ar[d, "\dom"'] \floor{J} & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=m}] \floor{J} \ar[d, "\dom"]\\
            A & \ar[l, loose, equal, ""{anchor=center,name=b}] A
            \ar[from=t, to=m, phantom, "\xi"{anchor=center}] \ar[from=m, to=b, phantom, "1_{\dom}"]
        \end{tikzcd}
        \]
        Where $x$ and $y$ are induced by $\alpha_1$ and $\alpha_0$ respectively using (T1).
\end{itemize}

In the case of double categories, this is written as ``Every commutative tetrahedron, factorizes through the tabulator":

\[\begin{tikzcd}[sep=large]
    \ar[d] X \ar[dr] & \ar[l, loose, "p"'] \ar[dl, crossing over] Y \ar[d]\\
    A & \ar[l, loose, "J"] B
\end{tikzcd}
\hspace{1em}=\hspace{1em}
\begin{tikzcd}
    \ar[dr] Y &{}& \ar[ll, loose, "p"'] X \ar[dl]\\
              & \ar[dl] \floor J \ar[dr] &\\
    A &{}& \ar[ll, loose, "J"] B
    \ar[from=1-2, to=2-2, phantom, "\xi"{anchor=center}]
    \ar[from=2-2, to=3-2, phantom, "\text{tab}"{anchor=center}]
\end{tikzcd}\]

There is also a dual notion of cotabulators. Both construct interesting objects:

\begin{example}
    In $\Dist$, the cotabulator is also known as the collage (gluing) along a distributor. The simplest example is for the terminal distributor $p : A\dfrom B$ defined as $p(a, b) = *$, the gluing of $A$ and $B$ along $p$ is the join $A \star B$.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[from a discussion with Jonas Frey]
    There is a virtual equipment $\bb{Log}$ of toposes, geometric morphisms and with distributors the left exact functors between the associated logoses. The cotabulator of $\Phi : \Sh(E) \to \Sh(F)$ is the \textit{Artin gluing} of $\Phi$.
\end{example}

The tabulators would have been more interesting in the context of this thesis, thanks to the following lemma:

\begin{lemma}
    If the tabulator of $X(f, g)$ exists, its object $\floor{X(f, g)}$ is the comma object $f\downarrow g$
\end{lemma}

In $\Dist$, when considering an adjunction $f : A \rightleftharpoons B : g$, a known property is that its envelope verifies $\floor{B(f, 1)} \simeqq \Env(f\dashv g) \simeqq f\downarrow g \simeqq \floor{A(1, g)}$. This is done using the explicit construction of all those categories. In a virtual equipment, for two distributors $p\simeqq q$, we have $\floor p \simeqq \floor q$ and this isomorphisms commutes with the $\dom$ and $\cod$ arrows.

It would be interesting to study the implications of an isomorphism $\floor p \simeqq \floor q$ commuting with $\dom$ and $\cod$. In a minimal virtual double category, it looks like it doesn't imply $p\simeqq q$. In fact, the axiom (T2) doesn't construct the cells we want, and although the intuition would be that there is a bijection of cells

\[\begin{iffseq}
    \floor p \to \floor q\\
    p \To q
\end{iffseq}\]

where the arrow $\floor p \to \floor q$ commute with $\dom$ and $\cod$, it seems this is not the case. The question left unanswered is the: is this bijection possible, and if not, what axiom should replace (T2) ?

This could have many implications, in particular computing relative adjunctions from tabulators or establishing the missing link between the fixed point of adjunctions and its envelope as written in \cite[Lemma 12.1]{averyleinster}, concluding our study of the Isbell adjunction in a virtual equipment.
